# Parity matrix: Python `scenerender` against the Rust `rs-scene-render`

Python `origin/main` 6431b8b; Rust checkout f296a4c (sr-core 1.1.3 + local 1.2 (SREPs 9-11, 13) + local 1.3 cinematic (srep-0000)). Phase 1 (measurement) of the feature-parity programme. Every row was derived by reading the Rust crate and the Python code; a second agent re-verified each area (all MISSING, DIVERGENT and PARTIAL rows, and a sample of PRESENT rows). Machine-readable twin: [parity-matrix.json](parity-matrix.json).

**Status:** PRESENT = same semantics; DIVERGENT = exists, semantics differ; PARTIAL = some sub-capabilities exist; MISSING = absent. **Size:** S <300 lines (<1 day) · M <1,500 lines (1-3 days) · L <6,000 lines or a new solver/format (1-2 weeks) · XL new subsystem, GPU path or >6,000 lines. **Impact** 1-5 (5 = nearly every scene). Validation/Schematron parity is tracked by the schema/validation work and only referenced here.

### Schema delta (what the Python schema lacks)

The Rust XSD (sha256 `f94906a93070`) is sr-core 1.1.3 plus the local 1.2 and 1.3 additions; the Python XSD (`3d0ecb499bc6`) is the older 1.1. 31 complex types and 17 simple types exist only in the Rust schema; 12 shared types gain attributes or child elements. Full lists are in `parity-matrix.json` under `schema_delta`. Complex types only in Rust: `basemapType`, `clayBlobType`, `craterType`, `erosionType`, `featureStyleType`, `flockType`, `fluidSourceType`, `fluidType`, `flyToType`, `fractureType`, `geoAssetType`, `geoLayerType`, `graticuleType`, `mapAssetType`, `mediumType`, `meshSequenceAssetType`, `oceanType`, `oceanWaveType`, `particles3DType`, `pinType`, `pyroImpulseType`, `pyroSourceType`, `pyroType`, `rigidBody3DType`, `routeType`, `segmentType`, `slimeType`, `tilesAssetType`, `volumeAssetType`, `waterImpulseType`, `whitewaterType`.

## Recommended implementation order

Dependencies first. Each step lists the areas and the rows that unblock the next steps.

1. **Schema, model, validation** (DOC; the parallel schema/validation work). Adopt the vendored 1.1.3 + 1.2 + 1.3 XSD/Schematron, version gates (V1-V7) and the typed-model/diagnostic parity. Everything schema-gated below depends on this.
2. **Evaluation semantics** (EVAL). Parameter/variant/data precedence, repeats/instances/includes scoping, timing and media clock, paint order, layout and safe areas, curves/expressions and the seeded-randomness contract. Compositor, effects and simulation read the evaluator, so divergences here propagate into every pixel.
3. **Compositor and colour** (COMP). Isolation rules, blend/mask/matte semantics, colour management and paints/generators; then FX (effects and transitions, adjustment layers, motion blur).
4. **Content areas that only need 1-3**: VEC, TEXT, 3D (+360, splats), MEDIA (decode/audio/delivery incl. output segments).
5. **Simulation and accessibility** (SIM): 3D rigid bodies, soft-body and particle parity, physics cache; then **interop** (INTEROP) and the **CLI/tooling** layer (CLI: quality tiers, changed-only, watch, strict reporting).
6. **New subsystems, each schema-gated and independent**: SIM2 (flock/fluid/slime/erosion, clay, screen-space lighting, path tracing), MAPS, RESOLVE, then CINE (volumes, pyro, ocean, fracture, mesh sequences, UHD path tracing), the last because it builds on 3D, SIM, SIM2 and MEDIA.

Within an area, take rows in descending impact then ascending size; the detail tables list `depends_on` for rows that need another row first.

## DOC — Document model, loading stages, validation structure, ID/reference model, version gates, explain/inspect

36 capabilities: 5 present, 7 divergent, 16 partial, 8 missing.

Not determined: Whether the Rust engine implements include/repeat/param expansion in sr-eval or elsewhere, and parity of those semantics (DOC-36).; Whether Python reports a dangling xs:IDREF for attributes not covered by an R-rule (libxml2 behaviour assumed from the Rust README).; Whether Python honours EXIF orientation for JPEG/PNG decode (no exif_transpose found; only TIFF orientation in image_io.py).; Whether any caller uses values.parse_timecode, and which 1.1 attributes are timecodeType.; Behavioural effect of missingFrame=error in Python (warns and draws nothing, not confirmed whether it aborts).; Exact validation time for a 10,000-node document in Python.; Rust-side meaning of the 'segments' document stage and sr-resolve (`scene-render resolve`), which touches A01/A02 help text but belongs to another area.; Python .sch has 87 assert ids vs Rust 185 (comm of the id sets); rule-level comparison is left to the validation parity work.

## COMP — GPU compositor core: isolation, blend modes, masks/mattes, colour management, paints & generators, 2.5D, caching, golden/diagnostics

45 capabilities: 17 present, 10 divergent, 11 partial, 7 missing.

Not determined: Whether Rust applies a threeD group node's own transform to its children (COMP-02); only render.rs push_quad for leaf quads was read.; Pixel-level equality of layer-edge antialiasing between cairo and the analytic shader (COMP-16) was not measured; no Rust renders were run.; Camera-log curve constants (S-Log3, LogC4, C-Log3, REDLog3G10, F-Log2, N-Log) were not compared digit by digit between color.rs and scenerender/color.py; Python's tests compare them against OCIO.; The Rust radial-gradient focal-radius formula versus cairo's two-circle gradient was analysed algebraically, not by rendering.; Dissolve behaviour inside nested offscreens (pixel origin) was reasoned from code, not rendered.; Cyclic matte references in Python (Rust reports an error without aborting).; Straddling-eye-plane behaviour of 2.5D layers in Python versus Rust's clip-space clipping.; Python orthographic camera, lens distortion and DoF on threeD layers have no Rust counterpart checked here (outside this area's Rust README range).

## FX — Effects (80 types), transitions (34+), adjustment layers, motion blur/time effects, quality tiers, colour finishing, custom GLSL shaders, edgeMode

45 capabilities: 13 present, 19 divergent, 10 partial, 3 missing.

Not determined: No pixel-level comparison between Python and Rust output was run (no cargo/GPU allowed); every DIVERGENT/PRESENT verdict comes from reading formulas, defaults and tests on both sides.; Whether Rust 'outline' (Entry::Morph op 2) differs from 'stroke' beyond the shared ring (fs_morph L1074-1138 only skimmed).; Bit-exact equality of seeded draws (Rust sr_eval::rng::element_seed purpose string '7.0' vs Python crc32 'seed:id:value:extra') for noise/film-grain/glitch/light-leak/vignette-like seeded transitions.; Python adjustment layers with time effects (echo/posterize-time) and Python behaviour for shutterAngle<=0 were not exercised.; Python effect rendering for rotated/scaled nodes was inferred from code (effects receive a frame-space raster, Params.d uses rc.scale only); not confirmed by an empirical render.; Rust 3D-object motion-blur pass and 3D DoF were out of scope (3D area).; Vignette half-diagonal for non-full-frame sized nodes (Rust uses the effect target size, Python the document frame) not rendered.; Rust transition 'luma' fallback behaviour without a matte vs Python's warning-and-wipe fallback not compared.; PyOpenColorIO and moderngl are present in the project venv, but whether headless GL works in the intended delivery environment (FX-27/FX-37) was not tested.

## VEC — Vector graphics, shape modifiers, SVG, Lottie, deformers, skeletons/IK/constraints, tracking data

47 capabilities: 16 present, 16 divergent, 14 partial, 1 missing.

Not determined: rlottie keyframed time remap (tm with keyframes) and mask feather/expansion were not probed; only a static tm was tested (not honoured).; Polystar roundness constants inside rlottie versus Rust were not compared pixel-wise.; Whether Python applies a deformer together with 2.5D (threeD) placement correctly, versus Rust drawing the layer undeformed, was not tested.; Python vs Rust exact trimOffset effect inside the repeater/trim modifier path was read from code only (modifiers.py uses degrees; shape-level uses turns).; librsvg is available here (HAVE_RSVG True) but the minimal fallback was not exercised against Rust for gradients.; The GPU rasteriser's pixel output was not run (cargo is forbidden); Rust claims come from code and test names.; Pattern-paint pixel parity (paint area box, transform) was not compared; the row only asserts support.; Whether Python tracking JSON with Rust-style rows could be auto-converted by sniffing was checked only for parse_json; format sniffing is by prefix, so no auto-conversion exists.; Rust node IK pivot detail (node_ik, rig.rs:699-727) was read only partially; the two-joint claim comes from its doc comment and the README.

## TEXT — Typography and data graphics: paragraph layout, fonts, colour glyphs, text animators/presets, captions, charts, audiograms, codes, formulas

44 capabilities: 20 present, 20 divergent, 1 partial, 3 missing.

Not determined: Whether D24 seed derivation for random order, wiggly noise and scramble letters yields byte-identical results between Rust (a.seed / selector seed) and Python (rc.ev.seed_for(an, channel), wiggly see…; COLR v0/v1 gradient and clip correctness and sbix handling through the installed Pango 1.52 and cairo 1.18; only a CBDT font (Noto Color Emoji) is installed here.; Whether pyphen en_US and the Rust `hyphenation` English patterns choose identical break points.; Thai/Khmer breaking in Pango (depends on libthai) versus Rust's per-character break.; Whether QR mask and version choice (segno vs rxing), Data Matrix symbol size (zxing-cpp vs rxing) and PDF417 row height produce identical module matrices.; Whether the Rust line box rule for lineHeight is the same tallest-run CSS rule that Python pins.; Whether the Rust wiggly selector's default rate equals Python's wiggleRate default of 2.0.; Whether `\over` and \mathbf are handled by the matplotlib mathtext fallback; on this machine the LaTeX engine is used, since pdflatex and pdftocairo are installed.; Exact Rust attribute mapping of chart `src` data (ChartData loader in sr-gpu/src/text.rs:916) against Python's CSV/JSON readers was not compared field by field.; No Rust build or run was available (read-only, no cargo), so no pixel comparison between the engines was made.

44 capabilities: 16 present, 17 divergent, 7 partial, 4 missing.

## MEDIA — Media, audio and delivery: decode, time controls/frame blending/optical flow, output segments (SREP 13), audio graph/effects/loudness/layouts, codecs/containers/HDR, hardware/2-pass, posters, destinations/uploads, parallel encode

57 capabilities: 16 present, 20 divergent, 9 partial, 12 missing.

Not determined: Whether Rust 'transfer_for' also maps every tag the README implies is true; only the six tags in video.rs were read.; EBU Tech 3341 conformance for both implementations (Rust test suite in sr-audio not opened; Python has no Tech 3341 test).; Numerical equivalence of K-weighting, true-peak oversampler and limiter between Rust and Python beyond the cited tests.; Rust behaviour of the 'hold' missingFrame policy when no earlier frame exists, and how Rust renders missingFrame=error (fails the whole render per README, not exercised).; Exact Rust default bar length for fitToDuration beyond the mix.rs fixed 4-beat bar at mix.rs:431; whether beatGrid beatsPerBar overrides it elsewhere.; Whether Python schema 1.1 permits container values flac/ogg/mka used by output.py.; Rust gamma/size thresholds for default YUV matrix (height>=720) versus Python (width>=1280 or height>576) only compared by reading code, not on edge-size files.; The sr-gpu output.rs letterbox implementation and EXR/NV12 shaders were not read line by line (README-level claims used).; Rust still-image decoding (icc.rs, heif.rs, still.rs, JPEG XL) is assumed covered by the images/assets area and was not compared.; Rust sequence output start_number/sequence_name handling versus Python's frame_%06d naming.

## INTEROP — Asset interop and colour: FBX animation stacks, binary USD (USDC/USDZ), OpenColorIO, still-image formats/orientation/ICC/H.273

31 capabilities: 10 present, 6 divergent, 8 partial, 7 missing.

Not determined: No Rust or Python code was run. All statuses come from reading the code.; Whether Python's ufbx evaluate_blend_weight (0-1) matches Rust's DeformPercent/100 on in-between channels was not checked, and no in-between FBX fixture exists.; Whether Python FBX applies geometry_to_node to skinned vertices the way Rust does (it keeps skinned vertices in geometry space) was not traced in _fbx_mesh.; Python GIF/APNG as video input (INTEROP-23) was not run. Video decode goes through ffmpeg, so it probably works.; Pillow DDS, PNM and ICO bit-depth and variant coverage versus the image crate was not tested. Rust crate coverage is read only from the README and module docs.; Python USD material network coverage (usd_materials.py) was taken from docs/USD.md and the tests listed there. The Rust side reads constants only.; Row ids go to INTEROP-31, with no Rust-side USD time-sample or reference-composition limits beyond those quoted from the README.

| ID | Capability | Rust evidence | Python status and evidence | Notes | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## SIM2 — 2D simulations (flock, fluid, slime, erosion), clay, screen-space lighting, cascaded shadows, path tracing

42 capabilities: 0 present, 4 divergent, 6 partial, 32 missing.

Not determined: Rust shadow shaders (three.wgsl) were not fully read: cascade blending, PCSS support and per-cascade bias are unverified.; pathtrace.wgsl radiance() was not read line by line: MIS between NEE and BSDF sampling, the exact firefly clamp value and the layer-plane sampling are taken from the README and test names.; Python status for SIM2-34 and SIM2-35 compares semantics from docstrings and code structure, not by rendering both.; Whether the Python GL context could support compute (GL 4.3) for a path tracer was not tested. Python's 3D renderer targets GLSL 4.1 per scene3d.py.; tools/evidence/*.json and the Rust golden fixtures for these features were not inspected.; The Rust SSR/AO shader parameter values (jitter pattern, step counts, fade distances) were not compared to any reference. Only the 16-sample AO count and 16-step contact march were confirmed.; clay collision-as-sphere behaviour lives in physics3d (another area) and was only noted.

## RESOLVE — Generated media and transcription: resolve step, request keys, store, providers, cache/digest contract, schema attributes

32 capabilities: 3 present, 2 divergent, 2 partial, 25 missing.

Not determined: Whether Rust model attributes on <generated> beyond Python's XSD generatedAssetType exist (not diffed); Exact byte-compat of Python-built request keys with Rust's serde serialisation (field order) - only relevant if stores are to be shared across engines; Whether Python's captions cache reader handles the per-word 'emphasis' flag; Rust render-time behaviour for non-JSON caption caches (srt/vtt) was not checked; Python accepts them; Python mixer internals were not read to confirm per-track post-fader signals are exposable for the transcriber input; Whisper/piper/audioforge availability and exact whisper.cpp output were not exercised (read-only, no ffmpeg in dev environment)

## Method and limits

- Rust capabilities were taken from the README (all sections), `RELEASE_NOTES.md`, `srep-0000-cinematic-impact.md`, `schema/UPSTREAM`, `crates/*/src`, and `tools/evidence/*.json`. Python status comes from reading `scenerender/`, `tests/` and `docs/`, not from registry labels.
- Rows whose Rust side is pending in the Rust acceptance ledger say so in their notes; Python cannot exceed Rust for them.
- The Rust `rapier`, `lyon`, `rustybuzz`, `naga`, `wgpu` and similar dependencies fix numerics that Python reproduces with different libraries (cairo, Pango, numpy, moderngl): rows marked DIVERGENT by design say where bit-for-bit agreement is neither possible nor required.
- Review pass: 92 corrections applied, 12 rows added after review. Items the inventory could not determine are listed per area above.
